[32m│[0m  [32mAnnual Outlook 2026: Teetering Resilience[0m                                                                                                                             [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPress Release[0m                                                                                                                                                         [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m2[0m                                                                                                                                                                     [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mminute read[0m                                                                                                                                                           [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mJanuary 16, 2026[0m                                                                                                                                                      [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mShare[0m                                                                                                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mCopy Link[0m                                                                                                                                                             [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mLink Copied[0m                                                                                                                                                           [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mClose[0m                                                                                                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m32%[0m                                                                                                                                                                   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mof executives expect more opportunities in the next year, and they’re already taking action to manage risk and adapt strategy.[0m                                        [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPwC Pulse Survey[0m                                                                                                                                                      [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mSurvey of 678 US executives, May 2025[0m                                                                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m53%[0m                                                                                                                                                                   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mof companies have moved beyond the planning phase on key actions like cost reduction, budget adjustments, and supplier diversification.[0m                               [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPwC Pulse Survey[0m                                                                                                                                                      [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mSurvey of 678 US executives, May 2025[0m                                                                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m90%[0m                                                                                                                                                                   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mof Health Industries executives cite cyber-attacks as a moderate or serious risk, the highest for those executives and the highest across all industries.[0m             [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPwC Pulse Survey[0m                                                                                                                                                      [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mSurvey of 678 US executives, May 2025[0m                                                                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m83%[0m                                                                                                                                                                   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mof executives say they’re adopting a more long-term, US-focused business strategy.[0m                                                                                    [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPwC Pulse Survey[0m                                                                                                                                                      [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mSurvey of 678 US executives, May 2025[0m                                                                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mGrowth is holding up but foundations are narrower, more concentrated, and increasingly exposed to risk[0m                                                                [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mAs the US and global economies head into 2026, growth remains intact, but the sources of that resilience are increasingly concentrated and fragile. According to [0m     [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPwC’s Annual Outlook 2026: Teetering Resilience , authored by US Chief Economist Dr. Alexis Crow, expansion continues—but depends heavily on a small set of forces [0m   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mthat will be tested by shifting financial conditions, policy choices and geopolitics.[0m                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPwC forecasts global GDP growth of 2.7% in 2026, broadly in line with 2025, reflecting continued resilience even as momentum remains uneven across regions. While [0m    [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mgrowth is holding up, it is increasingly reliant on a narrower set of drivers than in past cycles.[0m                                                                    [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m“The global economy has shown real staying power,” said Dr. Alexis Crow. “But resilience in 2026 isn’t automatic or evenly distributed. It relies on AI-driven [0m       [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32minvestment, supportive fiscal policy and confidence in financial markets—and each of those comes with new constraints.”[0m                                               [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mThe US outlook: AI investment and consumer strength[0m                                                                                                                   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mIn the US, investment tied to artificial intelligence continues to anchor growth. Capital spending on AI, data centers, power generation and digital infrastructure [0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mremains a key driver of economic activity. At the same time, the American consumer has proven resilient across income levels, with strong spending evident in both [0m   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mluxury retail and value-oriented chains. Against this backdrop, PwC forecasts that US economic growth will remain steady in 2026, with GDP growth of 2.1%.[0m            [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mPwC expects labor market conditions to remain relatively stable, with the unemployment rate hovering near 4.4% in 2026. Inflation dynamics, however, are becoming [0m    [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mincreasingly uneven. While an oversupplied oil market is likely to keep headline inflation contained, electricity prices are rising in parts of the country as power[0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mdemand surges—particularly from data center buildouts. According to the Energy Information Administration ,  residential electricity prices are forecast to rise by [0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m4.2% in 2026, while PwC projects  medical care costs will increase by about 8.5%, contributing to stickier core inflation and making the outlook more sensitive to [0m   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mmonetary policy decisions.[0m                                                                                                                                            [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mThe global picture: resilience, but unevenly shared[0m                                                                                                                   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mGlobally, resilience is evident—but not evenly distributed.[0m                                                                                                           [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mIn Europe, growth remains modest but stabilizing. PwC forecasts 0.9% growth in the eurozone in 2026, supported by public investment in defense and digital [0m           [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32minfrastructure, continued disbursement of EU recovery funds, and softer energy prices. Germany, Spain and Italy are expected to underpin regional performance, while[0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mconsumer spending in France has held up despite political uncertainty.[0m                                                                                                [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mEmerging market and developing economies continue to outperform advanced economies in growth terms, and have generated strong investment performance across asset [0m    [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mclasses. Improved macroprudential frameworks (such as reducing fiscal deficits, and bolstering stronger foreign exchange reserves) as well as the fostering of [0m       [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mdeeper regional trade ties are supporting expansion within certain EMs, and in regions such as Asia. India stands out as one of the fastest-growing major economies [0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32min 2026, with expected growth of around 6.7%, driven by high-tech exports and rising real wages and corresponding consumption activity.[0m                               [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mRisks beneath the surface[0m                                                                                                                                             [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mThis is where the concept of “teetering resilience” comes into focus.[0m                                                                                                 [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mEconomic growth in the US is increasingly reliant on a handful of sectors and easy financial conditions. Inflation pressures are shifting rather than disappearing, [0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mwhile record levels of public debt levels in advanced economies render bond markets in a fragile position. Currency movements, including a projected further [0m         [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mweakening of the US dollar, add another layer of complexity for businesses and investors navigating global markets – but also, opportunity.[0m                           [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32m“These are not classical ‘all boats overboard’ recession signals,” said Crow. “But they raise doubts about the breadth and durability of the current growth cycle. [0m   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mIn addition, certain policy shifts may have longer term negative impacts for the economy – about which markets remain stubbornly complacent.”[0m                         [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mFinancial stability and stablecoins[0m                                                                                                                                   [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mOne of the report’s central themes is the rapid expansion of stablecoins, which are moving into mainstream payment and cross-border transaction flows. Today, 99% of[0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mstablecoins are backed by the US dollar , creating a concentration risk at a time when the dollar itself is expected to soften further in 2026.[0m                       [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mWhile stablecoins may offer efficiency and cost advantages, their growing scale raises new considerations for financial stability. As issuers increase their [0m         [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mexposure to government debt markets and become more interconnected with the broader crypto ecosystem, transparency, regulation and reserve quality will play an [0m      [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mincreasingly important role in shaping outcomes for investors and policymakers.[0m                                                                                       [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mInvestment opportunities in the next phase of resilience[0m                                                                                                              [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mLooking ahead, the outlook highlights underappreciated dynamics that could shape the next phase of growth in both the US and globally.[0m                                [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mIn the US, the AI boom is reshaping real estate, with data centers emerging as a core asset class at the intersection of infrastructure, energy, and land use. [0m       [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mLooking beyond capital and energy inputs, investors and developers will do well to focus on human capital – that is, the labor required to build out data centers – [0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mthe supply of which is dented by current immigration policy in the US. Shortages of labor have also led to bottlenecks in data center developments in Japan.[0m          [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mWe also explore the prospects for de-dollarization in the US real estate market. So far, cross-border capital flows have held up well – but should the value of the [0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mdollar decline by another 10% in 2026, foreign investors may pause. This could open up an opportunity for USD domestic investors – and we highlight the development [0m  [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mof attainable housing – as well as retail – as two promising sectors for undaunted investors.[0m                                                                         [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mThe outlook also highlights Europe’s pensions gap as a perhaps overlooked business and investment opportunity. Private pension assets in the US are more than ten [0m    [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mtimes those in the euro area. Closing that gap has become increasingly critical for macroeconomic, demographic and geopolitical reasons, making 2026 a pivotal [0m       [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mmoment for Europe to take a page out of Wall Street’s book.[0m                                                                                                           [32m│[0m
[32m│[0m  [32mRead the full Annual Outlook 2026: Teetering Resilience[0m                                                                                                               [32m│[0m
